We notice that Lance Amano claims he was kidnapped and held in the kidnappers' hideout, and he testifies that his hands were cuffed but he was left alone, allowing him to escape (Testimony 3). However, there is evidence that the door to the hideout was locked (implied by the use of a broken prop sword to jam the handle from the inside, Evidence 12), and Lance also mentions that the door leading outside was locked tight, so he had to use the underground passageway (Testimony 5).

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction. 
The rule "If XXX is locked then XXX cannot be passed through" applies here because it indicates that if a door is locked, it cannot be passed through without being unlocked first.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
Given the rule about locked items, Lance's testimony about escaping through a locked door (by using an underground passageway because the main door was locked) seems to align with the evidence of the locked door. However, the contradiction arises when considering how Lance could have been left alone and managed to escape if the door was indeed locked and required an alternative route for escape. The direct contradiction, however, is more nuanced and relates to the specifics of Lance's escape and the state of the doors.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
Upon closer inspection, a more direct contradiction involves the testimonies about Lance's experience and the evidence related to his escape.
